**Q:** Which choice most logically completes the text?

- A) Consuming high doses of caffeine is the most reliable strategy for sustaining long-term focus on night shifts.
- B) Short, structured sleep interventions can offer more stable safety benefits during night shifts than chemical stimulants like caffeine.
- C) Factory operators should be completely barred from working any operational shifts after midnight.
- D) Mechanical tracking errors are entirely caused by the chemical interactions of caffeine within the human brain.

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The prompt asks which choice 'most logically completes the text,' but the passage ends with a complete sentence and contains no blank or lead-in clause (e.g., 'Therefore, ___'). Standard SAT inference items supply an incomplete final sentence for the answer to finish.

### R&W · Inferences · medium-high · #1 (q00421)

> In evolutionary biology, the "island syndrome" describes the tendency of small animal species isolated on islands to evolve into larger forms (island gigantism) due to a lack of apex predators and reduced interspecies competition. Conversely, large mammalian species on islands often evolve into much smaller forms (island dwarfism) because limited landmasses cannot provide enough food resources to sustain massive bodies. A team investigating fossil remains on an isolated Mediterranean island discovered both an ancestral rodent that grew to the size of a cat and an ancestral elephant that evolved to be no taller than a human.

**Q:** Which choice most logically completes the text?

- A) Rodents are evolutionary more advanced and capable of surviving resource scarcity than elephants are.
- B) The fossil discoveries provide empirical evidence that aligns with the dual ecological predictions of the island syndrome model.
- C) Elephants on the Mediterranean island underwent dwarfism primarily because they were hunted by giant island rodents.
- D) Island isolation uniformly forces all biological organisms to increase their physical body mass over evolutionary time.

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The prompt asks which choice 'most logically completes the text,' but the passage ends with a grammatically complete sentence and no blank or trailing clause (e.g., 'Thus, the researchers concluded that ___'). The conventional SAT setup for this item type is missing.
- difficulty_mismatch: The distractors are transparently wrong (A makes an unsupported 'more advanced' claim with a grammatical error, C invents rodent predation, D says 'uniformly all organisms increase mass,' contradicting the passage), making the item easier than the stated medium-high level.
- other: Choice A contains a usage error ('evolutionary more advanced' should be 'evolutionarily'), which is inappropriate in an Inferences item where options should be mechanically clean.

### R&W · Inferences · medium-high · #2 (q00422)

> A cognitive psychology experiment tested the problem-solving efficiency of teams operating under different supervisory constraints. Teams in Group 1 were required to submit progress reports to a manager every ten minutes, while teams in Group 2 were given an open three-hour window to complete the task autonomously before presenting their final results. While Group 1 generated highly organized, error-free documents that followed traditional guidelines, Group 2 developed radically innovative, out-of-the-box software solutions that solved the underlying logical prompt far more efficiently.

**Q:** Which choice most logically completes the text?

- A) Frequent managerial oversight can optimize procedural compliance but may inadvertently constrain creative, non-traditional innovation.
- B) Complete worker autonomy is guaranteed to cause a total collapse in document formatting and organizational structure.
- C) Group 2 would have been even more innovative if they had been completely barred from communicating with one another.
- D) Modern corporations should completely dismantle all middle-management positions to maximize technical efficiency.

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- formatting: The prompt asks which choice 'most logically completes the text,' but the passage contains no blank or incomplete final sentence; it ends as a complete statement. Standard SAT inference items end with an incomplete sentence (e.g., 'Therefore, the researchers concluded that ___').

### R&W · Inferences · medium-high · #3 (q00423)

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The item asks which choice 'most logically completes the text,' but the passage is a complete set of sentences with no blank or trailing clause (e.g., 'Therefore, ____') to be completed. SAT inference items require an explicit blank at the end of the text.

### R&W · Inferences · medium-high · #6 (q00426)

> An astrophysicist modeling the atmospheric retention of rocky exoplanets determined that a planet's ability to hold onto a thick atmosphere depends on a delicate balance between its surface gravity and its distance from its host star. High surface gravity prevents atmospheric gases from escaping into space, while close proximity to a star exposes the planet to intense stellar winds that strip gases away. The model predicts that an Earth-sized exoplanet orbiting very close to a highly active dwarf star will likely lose its atmosphere quickly, even if its initial gas volume was massive.

**Q:** Which choice most logically completes the text?

- A) Planet size is the only variable that determines whether an atmosphere can survive stellar winds.
- B) Massive initial gas volumes are sufficient to protect any exoplanet from atmospheric erosion.
- C) Intense stellar winds can override the atmospheric retention capabilities of an Earth-sized planet's surface gravity.
- D) Active dwarf stars are incapable of retaining rocky exoplanets within their gravitational orbits.

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- formatting: The prompt asks which choice "most logically completes the text," but the passage ends with a complete sentence and contains no blank or trailing incomplete clause (e.g., "This suggests that ___"). The item functions as an inference question rather than a completion item, so the stem and text are mismatched.
- difficulty_mismatch: The correct choice is a near-paraphrase of the passage's final sentence, and the three distractors are extreme/unsupported ('only variable,' 'any exoplanet,' 'incapable of retaining'). This is easier than the stated medium-high tier.

### R&W · Inferences · medium-high · #7 (q00427)

Conversely, in flat organizational structures with fewer management tiers, executives received failure updates directly from frontline workers within 24 hours.

**Q:** Which choice most logically completes the text?

- A) Middle managers in multi-tiered hierarchies are inherently malicious and seek to sabotage corporate profitability.
- B) Multi-tiered hierarchical structures can create barriers that delay and distort the upward transmission of critical operational data.
- C) Flat organizational structures guarantee that a company will never experience project failures.
