> - The bullet train's nose was redesigned to mimic the beak of the kingfisher bird, reducing the loud sonic boom it previously caused when exiting tunnels.
> - The redesign also reduced energy consumption by 15 percent and increased the train's speed by 10 percent.
> - Engineer Eiji Nakatsu, an avid birdwatcher, proposed the kingfisher-inspired redesign after observing how the bird enters water with minimal splash.

**Q:** The student wants to illustrate the practical benefits of biomimicry with a specific real-world example. Which choice most effectively uses the notes to accomplish this goal?

- A) Biomimicry is an approach to engineering and design that finds inspiration in natural structures and processes, with applications in fields ranging from transportation to architecture.
- B) Engineer Eiji Nakatsu was an avid birdwatcher who noticed that kingfisher birds can enter water with almost no splash, an observation that would eventually change the design of high-speed trains.
- C) By redesigning the bullet train's nose to mimic the kingfisher's beak, engineers eliminated a disruptive sonic boom while also cutting energy use by 15 percent and boosting speed by 10 percent.
- D) The kingfisher's beak is uniquely shaped to allow the bird to plunge into water with minimal resistance, a property that makes it an ideal model for engineers seeking to reduce drag in high-speed vehicles.

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- other: Minor mismatch with the notes: the note says the redesign 'reduc[ed] the loud sonic boom,' but the credited choice (C) says engineers 'eliminated' it. Authentic SAT synthesis items avoid overstating the notes; 'reduced' would be cleaner. This does not change which answer is best, since C is the only option that pairs a specific application with concrete practical benefits.

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #1 (q01079)

> The library closes at nine o'clock on weekdays _______ it stays open until midnight on Fridays and Saturdays.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard Written English?

- A) ,
- B) , but
- C) ; but,
- D) and,

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- other: Minor content inconsistency: Friday is itself a weekday, so saying the library closes at nine 'on weekdays' but stays open until midnight 'on Fridays' is logically contradictory. It doesn't affect the grammar being tested, but a polished item would use 'Monday through Thursday' or similar.

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #12 (q01090)

> The school garden is a popular spot for students during lunch. Tomatoes, peppers, and sunflowers grow in neat rows along the southern fence. A small sign near the entrance reminds visitors to water the plants gently and to avoid stepping on the roots. The students who tend the garden take great pride in watching things grow.

**Q:** Which choice best punctuates the underlined portion of the following sentence? "Tomatoes peppers and sunflowers grow in neat rows along the southern fence."

- A) Tomatoes peppers and sunflowers grow in neat rows along the southern fence.
- B) Tomatoes, peppers, and sunflowers grow in neat rows along the southern fence.
- C) Tomatoes, peppers and sunflowers, grow in neat rows along the southern fence.
- D) Tomatoes; peppers; and sunflowers, grow in neat rows along the southern fence.

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The stem refers to an "underlined portion," but no portion is underlined or otherwise marked; the answer choices instead reproduce the entire sentence. The mismatch is minor since the choices make the task clear.

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #13 (q01091)

> James decided to take a walk after dinner. The evening air was cool and fresh, and the neighborhood streets were quiet. He passed the old oak tree at the corner of Elm and Main, stopped for a moment to look up through the branches, and then continued on his way home.

**Q:** Which choice correctly punctuates the following sentence? "He passed the old oak tree at the corner of Elm and Main stopped for a moment to look up through the branches and then continued on his way home."

- A) He passed the old oak tree at the corner of Elm and Main, stopped for a moment to look up through the branches, and then continued on his way home.
- B) He passed the old oak tree at the corner of Elm and Main; stopped for a moment to look up through the branches; and then continued on his way home.
- C) He passed the old oak tree, at the corner of Elm and Main stopped for a moment to look up through the branches and then continued on his way home.
- D) He passed the old oak tree at the corner of Elm and Main, stopped for a moment to look up through the branches. And then continued on his way home.

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- other: The passage already contains the correctly punctuated sentence verbatim, so the item can be answered by matching rather than by applying any boundaries knowledge. Also, the format (quoting a sentence and asking which choice punctuates it correctly) differs from the standard Digital SAT convention of an underlined/blank portion within the passage.

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #14 (q01092)

> Keisha has three hobbies that keep her busy after school: painting, running, and reading. She sets aside at least one hour each day to focus on one of these activities. Her parents encourage her to pursue her interests because they believe that hobbies help children develop patience and creativity.

**Q:** Which choice correctly punctuates the following sentence? "Keisha has three hobbies that keep her busy after school painting running and reading."

- A) Keisha has three hobbies that keep her busy after school, painting, running, and reading.
- B) Keisha has three hobbies that keep her busy after school: painting, running, and reading.
- C) Keisha has three hobbies that keep her busy after school; painting, running, and reading.
- D) Keisha has three hobbies that keep her busy after school — painting running and reading.

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- other: The passage's first sentence is the correctly punctuated version of the target sentence, so the answer is given away verbatim; the item tests copying rather than knowledge of colon usage. Also, the question format (quoting a sentence and asking which choice punctuates it correctly) does not follow Digital SAT conventions, which embed a blank in the passage.

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #18 (q01096)

> The science fair is held every spring at Jefferson Middle School. Students from all six grade levels are invited to submit projects on topics of their choice. Last year's winning project explored how different types of soil affect plant growth. The judges look for creativity, careful research, and a clear presentation of results.

**Q:** Which choice correctly punctuates the following sentence? "Last year's winning project explored how different types of soil affect plant growth."

- A) Last year's winning project, explored how different types of soil affect plant growth.
- B) Last year's winning project explored, how different types of soil affect plant growth.
- C) Last year's winning project explored how different types of soil affect plant growth.
- D) Last year's winning project; explored how different types of soil affect plant growth.

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- other: The stem quotes the sentence in its already-correct form, which directly gives away the answer (choice C is verbatim identical to the quoted sentence). SAT Boundaries items normally underline a portion of a passage sentence and offer punctuation options rather than restating the correct version.

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #19 (q01097)

> Clara's favorite season is autumn. She loves the way the leaves change from green to red, orange, and gold. On weekends, she often visits the park near her home with a book and a thermos of hot apple cider. She says there is no better place to spend a quiet afternoon.

**Q:** Which choice correctly punctuates the following sentence? "She loves the way the leaves change from green to red orange and gold."

- A) She loves the way the leaves change from green to red orange and gold.
- B) She loves the way the leaves change from green, to red, orange, and gold.
- C) She loves the way the leaves change from green to red, orange, and gold.
- D) She loves the way the leaves change from green to red, orange, and gold — she says so often.

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- other: The passage itself contains the correctly punctuated sentence verbatim, so the answer is given away rather than tested; this also makes the item trivially easy and unlike authentic SAT Boundaries items, which embed the choice in a blank/underlined portion rather than asking 'which choice correctly punctuates.'

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #20 (q01098)

> The community center recently added a new computer lab with twenty workstations. Residents can use the computers for free during operating hours. A staff member is available to assist anyone who needs help navigating the internet, creating documents, or sending email. The lab has been very popular since it opened last month.

**Q:** Which choice correctly punctuates the following sentence? "A staff member is available to assist anyone who needs help navigating the internet creating documents or sending email."

- A) A staff member is available to assist anyone who needs help navigating the internet, creating documents, or sending email.
- B) A staff member is available, to assist anyone who needs help navigating the internet, creating documents, or sending email.
- C) A staff member is available to assist anyone; who needs help navigating the internet, creating documents, or sending email.
- D) A staff member is available to assist anyone who needs help navigating the internet creating documents or sending email.

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- other: The passage itself contains the target sentence already correctly punctuated, so a student can select choice A by simple matching without applying any knowledge of serial-comma conventions. Also, the format (a direct 'which choice correctly punctuates' prompt) is not the standard Digital SAT Boundaries format, which embeds a blank in the passage.

### R&W · Boundaries · easy · #23 (q01101)

> Penguins are remarkable birds that cannot fly. However, they are excellent swimmers. Their wings have evolved into flippers, these allow them to move through water with surprising speed and agility. Some species can swim at speeds of up to fifteen miles per hour.

**Q:** Which choice most effectively corrects the punctuation error in the underlined sentence?

- A) Their wings have evolved into flippers these allow them to move through water with surprising speed and agility.
- B) Their wings have evolved into flippers, they allow them to move through water with surprising speed and agility.
- C) Their wings have evolved into flippers; these allow them to move through water with surprising speed and agility.
- D) Their wings have evolved into flippers, and allowing them to move through water with surprising speed and agility.

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- formatting: The question refers to an "underlined sentence," but no underlining or other marking appears in the passage. The intended sentence is inferable from the answer choices, so the item is still answerable.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-low · #12 (q01120)

> Marine biologist Dr. Sylvia Earle has spent decades advocating for ocean conservation. Her research expeditions have taken her to some of the deepest, most remote parts of the sea. In 1979, she set a solo depth record by walking untethered on the ocean floor at 1,250 feet, a feat that remained unmatched for years.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of punctuation?

- A) feet, a feat that remained unmatched for years.
- B) feet. A feat that remained unmatched for years.
- C) feet a feat that remained unmatched for years.
- D) feet; a feat that remained unmatched for years.

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- formatting: The passage is presented with the punctuation already in place ("feet, a feat that remained unmatched for years") instead of containing a blank or underlined portion. This gives away the answer and makes the task of 'completing the text' incoherent.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-low · #13 (q01121)

> Urban heat islands form when cities replace natural land cover with pavement and buildings. These surfaces absorb more solar energy than vegetation does, releasing it as heat. Researchers have proposed several solutions, including the planting of trees along city streets, installing green roofs, and using reflective materials on buildings.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of punctuation?

- A) several solutions, including the planting
- B) several solutions: including the planting
- C) several solutions; including the planting
- D) several solutions including, the planting

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- formatting: The passage already contains the correct answer verbatim ("several solutions, including the planting") rather than presenting a blank or underlined segment, which gives the answer away and departs from standard SAT presentation.
- other: Minor: the list that follows is not parallel ("the planting of trees... installing green roofs... using reflective materials"), which is a distracting flaw in an item about punctuation.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-low · #16 (q01124)

> Linguist Noam Chomsky proposed that humans possess an innate capacity for language, which he called the language acquisition device. This idea, sometimes called universal grammar, suggests that all human languages share deep structural features. Critics, however, argue that language is learned entirely through environmental exposure and social interaction.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of punctuation?

- A) Critics however argue
- B) Critics, however argue,
- C) Critics, however, argue
- D) Critics; however, argue

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- formatting: The passage is printed with the punctuation already in place ("Critics, however, argue that...") rather than with a blank or underlined portion, so the answer is given away in the stimulus. The relevant sentence should be shown with an underlined/blank segment.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-low · #18 (q01126)

> James Baldwin's 1963 work The Fire Next Time remains one of the most powerful examinations of race in America. Composed of two essays, the book blends personal memoir with sweeping social commentary. Baldwin wrote with urgency and precision, his prose demanding that readers confront the moral consequences of racial injustice.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of punctuation?

- A) precision, his prose demanding
- B) precision his prose demanding
- C) precision; his prose demanding
- D) precision: his prose demanding

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- formatting: The passage already shows the final sentence fully punctuated as "precision, his prose demanding" instead of presenting a blank/underlined portion, so the correct answer (A) is given away in the text.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-low · #23 (q01131)

> Urban heat islands form when cities replace natural land cover with pavement and buildings, these surfaces absorb and retain heat more effectively than vegetation does. During the night, when rural areas cool down rapidly, city temperatures often remain several degrees warmer. This temperature difference can increase energy consumption, worsen air quality, and place additional stress on public health infrastructure.

**Q:** Which choice most effectively corrects the boundary error in the underlined portion?

- A) Urban heat islands form when cities replace natural land cover with pavement and buildings. These surfaces absorb and retain heat more effectively than vegetation does.
- B) Urban heat islands form when cities replace natural land cover with pavement and buildings, so these surfaces absorb and retain heat more effectively than vegetation does.
- C) Urban heat islands form when cities replace natural land cover with pavement and buildings, although these surfaces absorb and retain heat more effectively than vegetation does.
- D) Urban heat islands form when cities replace natural land cover with pavement and buildings; they absorb and retain heat more effectively than vegetation does, these surfaces.

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- formatting: The question refers to an "underlined portion," but no portion of the passage is underlined or otherwise marked; the student must infer that the comma splice in the first sentence is the target. The answer is still determinable, but the cue is missing.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-low · #25 (q01133)

> The baobab tree, sometimes called the "tree of life," can store thousands of liters of water in its thick trunk. This adaptation helps the tree survive in the arid savannas of Africa and Madagascar. Local communities rely on the baobab for food, medicine, and shelter; making it one of the most culturally important trees on the continent.

**Q:** Which choice most effectively corrects the boundary error in the underlined portion?

- A) Local communities rely on the baobab for food, medicine, and shelter, making it one of the most culturally important trees on the continent.
- B) Local communities rely on the baobab for food, medicine, and shelter. Making it one of the most culturally important trees on the continent.
- C) Local communities rely on the baobab for food, medicine, and shelter: making it one of the most culturally important trees on the continent.
- D) Local communities rely on the baobab for food, medicine, and shelter, and making it one of the most culturally important trees on the continent.

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- formatting: The question refers to an "underlined portion," but no text in the passage is actually underlined. The intended portion is inferable from the answer choices, so the item is still answerable, but the underlining should be restored.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-low · #26 (q01134)

> Psychologist Carol Dweck's research on mindset has influenced educators around the world. Her studies found that students who believe their abilities can grow through effort — those with a "growth mindset" — consistently outperform students who view intelligence as a fixed trait. Schools in several countries have since adopted Dweck's framework to redesign how teachers provide feedback, encourage persistence, and assess student progress.

**Q:** Which choice identifies and corrects a boundary error if one is present, or confirms that the passage requires no correction?

- A) Psychologist Carol Dweck's research on mindset has influenced educators around the world, her studies found that students who believe their abilities can grow through effort consistently outperform students who view intelligence as a fixed trait.
- B) Schools in several countries have since adopted Dweck's framework to redesign how teachers provide feedback, encourage persistence, and assess student progress; this is an important development.
- C) Her studies found that students who believe their abilities can grow through effort — those with a "growth mindset" — consistently outperform students who view intelligence as a fixed trait, this is well established.
- D) The passage as written contains no boundary errors and requires no correction.

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- other: The item format is nonstandard for the Digital SAT: Boundaries questions normally present a blank within the text with four punctuation options rather than asking students to select among full rewritten sentences. Still answerable, since only D leaves the correct original intact (A and C introduce comma splices; B adds an unnecessary clause and is not a 'correction').

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-high · #6 (q01144)

> The council voted to approve the new zoning ordinance _______ despite objections from several neighborhood groups who argued that increased density would worsen traffic and strain local infrastructure.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard Written English?

- A) ;
- B) ,
- C) : and
- D) . Despite

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The item is easier than 'medium-high': a semicolon and a period both require an independent clause (only the prepositional phrase 'despite objections...' follows), and ': and' is not a conventional construction, so three choices are eliminated almost instantly. Also, strictly speaking no punctuation at all would be the most natural choice before 'despite'; the comma is merely the only permissible option offered.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-high · #11 (q01149)

> The Mayan calendar system, which consisted of interlocking cycles of varying lengths, has fascinated historians and astronomers alike for centuries. The most studied of these cycles, the 365-day Haab, closely approximated the solar year. Scholars have long debated whether the Maya intentionally accounted for the fractional day left over each year, a debate that remains unresolved despite decades of archaeological inquiry and careful analysis of surviving codices from the classical period.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of sentence boundaries?

- A) The most studied of these cycles the 365-day Haab, closely approximated the solar year.
- B) The most studied of these cycles, the 365-day Haab closely approximated the solar year.
- C) The most studied of these cycles, the 365-day Haab, closely approximated the solar year.
- D) The most studied of these cycles: the 365-day Haab, closely approximated the solar year.

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- formatting: The passage includes the sentence in its fully punctuated form rather than leaving a blank or underlined portion; choice C is reproduced verbatim in the stimulus, which gives away the answer and makes the 'completes the text' prompt incoherent.
- difficulty_mismatch: A standard appositive-comma-pair item, with the answer already shown in the passage, is well below the stated medium-high difficulty.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-high · #13 (q01151)

> Urban heat islands, metropolitan areas that experience significantly higher temperatures than surrounding rural regions due to human activity and infrastructure, pose considerable challenges for city planners. Heat-absorbing surfaces such as asphalt and concrete, combined with reduced vegetation cover, trap solar energy and elevate nighttime temperatures. - B) Heat-absorbing surfaces such as asphalt and concrete, combined with reduced vegetation cover, trap solar energy and elevate nighttime temperatures.

- D) Heat-absorbing surfaces, such as asphalt and concrete combined with reduced vegetation cover, trap solar energy and elevate nighttime temperatures.

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The item has no blank or underlined portion: the passage already contains the sentence in question, and choice B reproduces it verbatim, so the correct answer is given away in the stimulus. SAT Boundaries items present the sentence with a blank (or the choices as alternatives to a marked portion).
- difficulty_mismatch: Because the passage states the correctly punctuated version, the item is far easier than its stated medium-high difficulty; test-takers can match the text without reasoning about the nonessential modifier.

- formatting: The passage already contains the completed sentence verbatim instead of presenting a blank or underlined portion, so the item gives away the answer (choice B is a word-for-word copy of the passage's final sentence). ### R&W · Boundaries · medium-high · #17 (q01155)

- formatting: The passage reproduces the target sentence in full rather than presenting a blank or an underlined portion, so the correct answer (D) is given verbatim in the stimulus. Standard SAT Boundaries items omit the relevant text and ask the student to supply it.
- difficulty_mismatch: Because the correct punctuation is already shown in the passage, the item is trivially solvable by matching, well below the stated medium-high difficulty.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-high · #18 (q01156)

> The Great Oxygenation Event, which occurred approximately 2.4 billion years ago, transformed Earth's atmosphere from one dominated by reducing gases to one rich in free oxygen. This shift, driven largely by the photosynthetic activity of cyanobacteria, proved catastrophic for most anaerobic organisms that had thrived in the oxygen-free environment. In a striking irony, the metabolic byproduct of one group of organisms became the very agent of mass extinction for countless others.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of sentence boundaries?

- A) This shift driven largely by the photosynthetic activity of cyanobacteria, proved catastrophic for most anaerobic organisms that had thrived in the oxygen-free environment.
- B) This shift, driven largely by the photosynthetic activity of cyanobacteria proved catastrophic for most anaerobic organisms that had thrived in the oxygen-free environment.
- C) This shift, driven largely by the photosynthetic activity of cyanobacteria, proved catastrophic for most anaerobic organisms that had thrived in the oxygen-free environment.
- D) This shift, driven largely by the photosynthetic activity of cyanobacteria, proved catastrophic, for most anaerobic organisms that had thrived in the oxygen-free environment.

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- formatting: The passage already contains the sentence to be revised in its correct form (identical to choice C) rather than presenting a blank or underlined portion. This both gives away the answer and makes the task of 'completing the text' unclear.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-high · #19 (q01157)

> Historians of early modern Europe have long debated the extent to which the printing press, introduced to Europe by Johannes Gutenberg in the mid-fifteenth century, accelerated the Protestant Reformation. Some argue that Luther's theses spread with unprecedented speed precisely because of print technology, a medium that allowed ideas to proliferate far beyond the reach of church authorities. Others contend that the social and theological conditions for reform were already so ripe that the Reformation would have occurred regardless of the press's existence.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of sentence boundaries?

- A) Some argue that Luther's theses spread with unprecedented speed precisely because of print technology, a medium that allowed ideas to proliferate far beyond the reach of church authorities.
- B) Some argue that Luther's theses spread with unprecedented speed, precisely because of print technology, a medium that allowed ideas to proliferate far beyond the reach of church authorities.
- C) Some argue that Luther's theses spread with unprecedented speed precisely because of print technology; a medium that allowed ideas to proliferate far beyond the reach of church authorities.
- D) Some argue that Luther's theses spread with unprecedented speed precisely because of print technology a medium that allowed ideas to proliferate, far beyond the reach of church authorities.

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- formatting: The passage contains no blank or underlined portion; instead it reproduces the target sentence verbatim in its correct form, which both makes the task unclear and gives away choice A as the answer.
- difficulty_mismatch: Because the correct punctuation appears in the passage itself, the item is trivially easy rather than medium-high; the distractors (an unpunctuated run-together in D, a semicolon before a fragment in C) are also easily eliminated.

### R&W · Boundaries · medium-high · #20 (q01158)

Scientists emphasize, however, that the precise timeline for such changes depends heavily on the rate of future greenhouse gas emissions.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of sentence boundaries?

- A) Recent models suggest that even a partial collapse of the West Antarctic Ice Sheet a region long considered particularly vulnerable due to its marine-based geology, could raise global sea levels by several meters over the coming centuries.
- B) Recent models suggest that even a partial collapse of the West Antarctic Ice Sheet, a region long considered particularly vulnerable due to its marine-based geology, could raise global sea levels by several meters over the coming centuries.

- D) Recent models suggest that even a partial collapse of the West Antarctic Ice Sheet, a region long considered particularly vulnerable, due to its marine-based geology, could raise global sea levels by several meters over the coming centuries.

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The passage contains no blank; instead it reproduces the target sentence in full, with the correct punctuation already in place. Standard SAT Boundaries items present the sentence with a blank (or underlined portion) to be completed.
- difficulty_mismatch: Because the correctly punctuated sentence appears verbatim in the passage, a student can select choice B by simple matching rather than by applying punctuation rules. This makes the item far easier than the stated medium-high difficulty.

### R&W · Boundaries · hard · #11 (q01179)

> The philosopher Hannah Arendt argued that totalitarianism was not merely an extreme form of tyranny but something genuinely novel in human history. Traditional despotisms, however brutal, had limited ambitions: they sought to control behavior, not to remake human nature itself. Totalitarian regimes, by contrast, pursued total domination, the eradication of spontaneity, individuality, and even the capacity for thought. Arendt's analysis, drawing on both the Nazi and Stalinist experiences, suggested that this unprecedented ambition was what made twentieth-century totalitarianism so philosophically and morally distinct from all earlier forms of oppression.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text with the most logical and grammatically correct use of sentence boundaries?

- A) Traditional despotisms, however brutal, had limited ambitions: they sought to control behavior, not to remake human nature itself, totalitarian regimes, by contrast, pursued total domination.
- B) Traditional despotisms, however brutal, had limited ambitions: they sought to control behavior, not to remake human nature itself. Totalitarian regimes, by contrast, pursued total domination, the eradication of spontaneity, individuality, and even the capacity for thought.
- C) Traditional despotisms, however brutal, had limited ambitions: they sought to control behavior, not to remake human nature itself; totalitarian regimes by contrast pursued total domination, the eradication of spontaneity, individuality, and even the capacity for thought.
- D) Traditional despotisms, however brutal, had limited ambitions, they sought to control behavior, not to remake human nature itself. Totalitarian regimes, by contrast, pursued total domination: the eradication of spontaneity, individuality, and even the capacity for thought.

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The item has no blank or underlined portion; the passage as given already contains the exact wording of choice B, so a student can answer by simple matching rather than by analyzing sentence boundaries. Standard SAT Boundaries items present the sentence(s) with an underlined/blank segment and omit the correct punctuation from the stimulus.
- difficulty_mismatch: Because the correct version is printed verbatim in the passage and the distractors contain obvious comma splices, the item is far easier than the stated 'hard' tier.
- other: Choice C's defect (omitting the commas around the parenthetical 'by contrast') is a comma-usage issue rather than a sentence-boundary issue, slightly off-skill for the question stem, though it is still clearly inferior to B.

- C) premise that the mind cannot be fully understood through its material constituents alone, her critics
- D) premise: that the mind cannot be fully understood through its material constituents alone. Her critics

- C) waters. These findings challenging long-standing assumptions

Recent neuroimaging data, however, complicate this tidy partition by revealing that the boundaries between these proposed components are considerably more porous than the model implies.

- formatting: The passage is presented in full with no blank or underlined portion, so the student cannot see which segment the choices are meant to replace; the item relies on the reader inferring that the clause 'subsystems. Recent neuroimaging data, however, complicate...' is the tested portion.
- difficulty_mismatch: For a 'hard' Boundaries item, the distractors are unusually transparent (one plain comma splice and two with obviously omitted commas around 'however'), making the correct choice easy to spot without close reading.

### R&W · Boundaries · hard · #30 (q01198)

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- formatting: The passage already contains the complete target sentence verbatim (identical to choice C), so there is no blank or underlined portion to complete. The second sentence of the passage should be replaced with a blank/underline for the item to function as written.
- difficulty_mismatch: Despite the dense academic vocabulary, the grammatical task (setting off the interrupting clause 'she argues' with paired commas) is straightforward, and the distractors are easily eliminated; this is closer to medium difficulty than hard.

### R&W · Form, Structure, and Sense · medium-high · #21 (q01279)

### R&W · Form, Structure, and Sense · hard · #1 (q01289)

### R&W · Form, Structure, and Sense · hard · #11 (q01299)

> The philosopher's argument, though initially compelling, rests on a distinction that few scholars have examined with sufficient rigor. She contends that moral obligations arising from voluntary agreements differ categorically from those arising from circumstance — a position that, if accepted, would dramatically narrow the scope of redistributive justice. Critics, however, point out that the line between chosen and unchosen situations is rarely as clear as she assumes; individuals who appear to have chosen freely often do so under conditions of constrained options that render the notion of genuine voluntariness deeply questionable.

- A) render the notion of genuine voluntariness deeply questionable.

- C) have rendered the notion of genuine voluntariness to be deeply questionable.
- D) would render the notion of genuine voluntariness as deeply questionable.

- formatting: The passage already contains the answer text ("render the notion of genuine voluntariness deeply questionable") rather than a blank or an underlined portion, so the item does not actually present a gap to be completed.

### R&W · Form, Structure, and Sense · hard · #14 (q01302)

> Economists studying wage stagnation have increasingly turned their attention to the phenomenon of monopsony — a labor market condition in which a single employer, or a small coalition of employers acting in concert, possesses sufficient market power to suppress wages below competitive equilibrium levels. Unlike the more familiar concept of monopoly, in which a seller controls price, monopsony inverts this logic: here, the buyer of labor _______ the power to set terms unilaterally, leaving workers with limited recourse short of exiting the labor market entirely, an option that is itself constrained by geographic immobility and specialized skill sets.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English?

- A) are wielding
- B) wield
- C) wields
- D) have wielded

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The underlying task is a straightforward subject-verb agreement check ('the buyer of labor' = singular), with three distractors all plural; the dense economics vocabulary raises reading load but not grammatical difficulty, so 'hard' is somewhat generous.

### R&W · Form, Structure, and Sense · hard · #21 (q01309)

> The philosopher's central argument—that moral obligations arise not from abstract principles but from the particular relationships one sustains with others—has been contested on the grounds that it renders ethics ungeneralizable. Critics argue that if obligations depend entirely on relational context, then no universal moral claim can be made. Yet the philosopher herself maintained that such particularity is not a flaw but a feature: to demand that ethics transcend the local and contingent is to misunderstand the nature of moral life, which is always already embedded in specific communities, histories, and bonds of care.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English? The philosopher's central argument—that moral obligations arise not from abstract principles but from the particular relationships one _______ with others—has been contested on the grounds that it renders ethics ungeneralizable.

- A) sustains
- B) sustaining
- C) have sustained
- D) to sustain

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Although the passage is dense, the grammar task itself (choosing a finite verb agreeing with the singular subject 'one' in the relative clause) is straightforward; the distractors are a participle, an infinitive, and a plural verb, none of which is genuinely tempting. This reads as an easy/medium item rather than 'hard.'

### R&W · Form, Structure, and Sense · hard · #26 (q01314)

> In the decades following the Civil War, a cohort of American women reformers—among them settlement house founders, temperance advocates, and labor organizers—began to articulate a vision of civic life in which women's domestic expertise was not a disqualification from public life but a qualification for it. They argued that the skills honed in managing households and caring for families were precisely the skills the industrial city required: attentiveness to health, an understanding of poverty's structural causes, and the capacity to build institutions that could sustain vulnerable communities over time.

**Q:** Which choice completes the text so that it conforms to the conventions of Standard English? They argued that the skills honed in managing households and caring for families _______ precisely the skills the industrial city required.

- A) was
- B) is
- C) are
- D) were

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- formatting: The passage itself already contains the completed sentence with 'were' ('were precisely the skills the industrial city required'), so the blank is not blanked out in the stimulus and the answer is given away. The second paragraph should read 'They argued that the skills honed in managing households and caring for families _______ precisely the skills...'
- difficulty_mismatch: Labeled 'hard,' but once the number of the subject ('skills') is identified, only two plural options remain and the past-tense context ('argued,' 'required') makes 'were' straightforward; this is a routine easy/medium agreement-and-tense item.

### Math · Algebra · easy · #26 (q01344)

**Q:** A gym charges a $40 sign-up fee plus $15 per month. Which expression represents the total cost, in dollars, after m months?

- A) 40 + 15m
- B) 15 + 40m
- C) 40m + 15

### Math · Algebra · hard · #5 (q01443)

- A) 2

- C) 4
- D) 5

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The equation factors immediately as (x^2-1)(x^2-4)=0, giving x = ±1, ±2. This is a routine medium-level quadratic-in-form item rather than a hard one; the answer choices also don't include plausible hard-level distractors (e.g., 0 or 1).

### Math · Algebra · hard · #36 (q01474)

**Q:** If f(x) = 3x - 2, what is the value of f^-1(7), where f^-1 is the inverse function of f?

- A) 1
- B) 2
- C) 3
- D) 4

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Solving 3x - 2 = 7 is a single-step linear equation; this is an easy-tier item, not hard.

### Math · Algebra · hard · #37 (q01475)

**Q:** A rectangle's length is represented by (x+8) and its width by (x-3). If the area is 12, what is a possible positive value of x?

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 5
- D) 6

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Expanding (x+8)(x-3)=12 to x^2+5x-36=0 and factoring to x=4 is a routine medium-level task; also, the answer can be found by quick substitution of the four choices, making it easier than a 'hard' item.

### Math · Algebra · hard · #39 (q01477)

**Q:** For what value of k does 5(x-2) - 3(x+1) = 2x - k have infinitely many solutions?

- A) -13
- B) -3
- C) 3
- D) 13

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The item only requires distributing and combining like terms, then matching constants (k = 13). This is a routine easy/medium task, not a 'hard' tier problem.

### Math · Data Analysis · easy · #8 (q01486)

**Q:** Which measure of center is most affected by an extreme outlier in a data set?

- A) Mean
- B) Median
- C) Mode
- D) Range

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- other: Minor style issue: the Digital SAT typically tests this concept with an actual data set or scenario rather than as an abstract definition question. Also, choice D (Range) is a measure of spread, not center, making it a weak distractor.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-low · #8 (q01526)

**Q:** A number is increased by 20% and then the result is decreased by 20%. If the original number is 50, what is the final value?

**Key:** 48 · **Reviewer:** 48 (high)
- other: Skill label 'Data Analysis' is a poor fit; this is a percent-change problem (Problem Solving and Data Analysis loosely, but really percentages).

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-low · #14 (q01532)

**Q:** Using the same survey (200 respondents, 120 employed, 90 of the employed have a college degree), what percent of employed respondents have a college degree?

**Key:** 75 · **Reviewer:** 75 (high)
- formatting: The stem opens with "Using the same survey," referring to a prior question that is not included. All needed numbers are restated, so the item is still solvable, but the dangling reference should be removed for a standalone item.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #1 (q01559)

**Q:** Of 200 students, 90 are freshmen and 110 are not freshmen. Of the freshmen, 54 play a sport. If 100 students total play a sport, how many non-freshmen play a sport?

- A) 36
- B) 40
- C) 46
- D) 56

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Single subtraction (100 − 54); easier than a medium-high item.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #3 (q01561)

**Q:** A bag contains 4 red and 6 blue marbles. If two marbles are drawn at random without replacement, what is the probability that both are red?

- A) 1/15
- B) 2/15
- C) 1/5
- D) 4/15

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a standard, straightforward dependent-probability computation; it is closer to easy/medium than medium-high.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #4 (q01562)

**Q:** A game costs $5 to play. A player wins $20 with probability 0.1 and wins nothing otherwise. What is the expected net gain or loss per game?

- A) -$5
- B) -$3
- C) $0
- D) $2

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The computation (0.1 × $20 = $2; $2 − $5 = −$3) is a single-step expected-value calculation, which is easier than the stated medium-high tier. Also, expected value is not a standard Digital SAT Data Analysis topic.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #5 (q01563)

**Q:** A student's overall grade is a weighted average of a test (60% of the grade) and a project (40% of the grade). If the project score was 90 and the overall grade is 84, what was the test score?

**Key:** 80 · **Reviewer:** 80 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: A one-step weighted-average equation is easier than the stated medium-high tier; most students solve this in a single step.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #6 (q01564)

**Q:** A population's data distribution is skewed right (has a long tail toward higher values). Which statement is generally true?

- A) The mean is less than the median
- B) The mean is greater than the median
- C) The mean equals the median
- D) The relationship cannot be determined

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct recall of a standard fact (right skew → mean pulled above median) with no computation or data interpretation; it is an easy-tier item rather than medium-high.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #9 (q01567)

**Q:** A survey of 400 people found that 240 favor a proposal. A separate survey of 100 different people found that 55 favor the same proposal. Which statement is best supported by this information?

- A) The two surveys give identical estimates
- B) The larger survey's estimate of 60% is generally more precise due to its larger sample size
- C) The smaller survey is always more accurate
- D) Both surveys must be biased since their results differ

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The reasoning (larger sample = more precise) is conceptual and easier than a typical medium-high Data Analysis item; distractors contain absolute words ('always,' 'must') that make elimination trivial.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #10 (q01568)

**Q:** A data set is given: 3, 7, 7, 7, 10, 15, 50. How does the value 50 affect the mean compared to the median?

- A) It has no effect on either measure
- B) It significantly raises the mean but has little effect on the median
- C) It significantly raises both measures equally
- D) It lowers the mean

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a conceptual recall item about outliers affecting the mean more than the median; it requires essentially no computation and is far easier than a 'medium-high' SAT Data Analysis question.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #11 (q01569)

**Q:** For the data set 3, 7, 7, 7, 10, 15, 50, what is the median?

**Key:** 7 · **Reviewer:** 7 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Finding the median of a 7-value, already-sorted list is a basic (easy) task, well below the stated medium-high difficulty; no distractor structure, outlier reasoning, or multi-step work is required.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #12 (q01570)

**Q:** Two standard six-sided dice are rolled. What is the probability that the sum of the two dice is 7?

- A) 1/12
- B) 1/9
- C) 1/6
- D) 1/4

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a standard, very commonly memorized probability fact (6 favorable outcomes out of 36 = 1/6). It is an easy-tier item, not medium-high.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #15 (q01573)

**Q:** A survey of 100 people rates satisfaction on a scale of 1 to 5, with the following distribution: 1: 5 people, 2: 10 people, 3: 20 people, 4: 40 people, 5: 25 people. What is the mean satisfaction rating?

- A) 3.3
- B) 3.5
- C) 3.7
- D) 3.9

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward weighted-mean computation (370/100 = 3.7) with no twist; it is an easy-level item rather than medium-high.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #16 (q01574)

**Q:** A distribution has mean, median, and mode that are all equal. Which shape best describes this distribution?

- A) Skewed left
- B) Skewed right
- C) Symmetric
- D) Bimodal

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a recall-level fact (mean = median = mode suggests symmetry) with no computation or data interpretation required; it is substantially easier than a 'medium-high' SAT Data Analysis item.
- other: Strictly speaking, equality of mean, median, and mode does not guarantee symmetry (counterexamples exist), so the stem would be better phrased as 'best describes' with a typical/approximate qualifier — though the intended answer is unambiguous by SAT convention.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #17 (q01575)

**Q:** A recipe uses 2 cups of flour to make 10 servings. If the recipe is scaled up to make 35 servings while maintaining the same ratio, how many cups of flour are needed?

**Key:** 7 · **Reviewer:** 7 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step proportion (2/10 = x/35) with an integer answer; it is an easy-tier item, not medium-high.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #18 (q01576)

**Q:** A bag has red, blue, and green balls in the ratio 2:3:5. If there are 50 balls total, how many are blue?

- A) 10
- B) 15
- C) 20
- D) 25

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward one-step ratio computation (parts sum to 10, scale factor 5, blue = 15). It is an easy-tier item, not medium-high difficulty.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #21 (q01579)

**Q:** A sample of 60 students has test scores with a mean of 75 and a standard deviation of 10. If every student's score is increased by 5 points due to a curve, what is the new standard deviation?

- A) 5
- B) 10
- C) 15
- D) Cannot be determined

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Adding a constant to every value leaves the standard deviation unchanged — a single-step conceptual fact typically tested at the easy/medium level, not medium-high.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #23 (q01581)

**Q:** Using the same jar (balls numbered 1-10), what is the probability that the first ball drawn is even and the second ball drawn (without replacement) is odd?

**Key:** 5/18 · **Reviewer:** 5/18 (high)
- other: The phrase 'Using the same jar' references a prior question, though the parenthetical (balls numbered 1-10) makes the item self-contained; minor stylistic issue only.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #24 (q01582)

**Q:** A survey suffers from systematic non-response bias when:

- A) Every member of the population has an equal chance of responding
- B) Certain groups are less likely to respond, skewing the results
- C) The sample size is very large
- D) The survey is conducted online

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward definition-recall item; the correct choice is obvious and the distractors are not plausible. It is far easier than a 'medium-high' SAT Data Analysis question, which would typically require interpreting a specific survey scenario or computing/evaluating generalizability.
- other: Format is unlike actual SAT Math items: the SAT does not test vocabulary definitions in isolation (e.g., 'non-response bias') and instead embeds sampling concepts in a described study with data.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #25 (q01583)

**Q:** If the mean of a data set is 60 and every value in the data set is multiplied by 2, what is the new mean?

- A) 60
- B) 62
- C) 90
- D) 120

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step application of a basic property of the mean (scaling all values by 2 scales the mean by 2). It is an easy-tier item, not medium-high; the distractors (62, 90) are not plausible traps for any common misconception.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #26 (q01584)

**Q:** A data set has a standard deviation of 5. If every value is multiplied by 2, what is the new standard deviation?

**Key:** 10 · **Reviewer:** 10 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step scaling fact about standard deviation; it is easier than a 'medium-high' difficulty item. Also, standard deviation transformation rules are outside the typical Digital SAT content scope (SAT treats standard deviation only conceptually/qualitatively).

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #27 (q01585)

**Q:** A study compares two teaching methods by randomly assigning students to each method. This design allows researchers to conclude:

- A) Correlation only, not causation
- B) Causation, since random assignment controls for confounding variables
- C) Nothing meaningful without a much larger sample size
- D) That the two methods are equally effective

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward conceptual recall item about random assignment; the distractors are weak (C and D are obviously wrong), making it easier than a 'medium-high' difficulty item.
- other: The stem lacks any data or statistical result; it tests only a definition rather than the data-analysis reasoning typical of SAT items, which usually embed such questions in a described study with results.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #28 (q01586)

**Q:** A quality control inspector checks a random sample of 50 out of 1,000 manufactured parts and finds 3 defective. Based on this sample, about how many defective parts would be expected in the full batch of 1,000?

- A) 30
- B) 45
- C) 60
- D) 75

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward one-step proportion (3/50 × 1000); it is easier than a typical medium-high difficulty data analysis item.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #31 (q01589)

**Q:** In an experiment, a treatment group shows improvement compared to a control group, but participants were not randomly assigned—they chose which group to join. Which is a valid concern about this result?

- A) The results cannot show any correlation at all
- B) Self-selection bias may confound the results
- C) The sample size is definitely too small to be useful
- D) There is no possible concern with this design

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The item is labeled medium-high but is conceptually trivial: three distractors are absolute/implausible statements ('cannot show any correlation at all', 'definitely too small', 'no possible concern'), making B identifiable without real statistical reasoning. Actual SAT medium-high inference items typically require interpreting data or quantifying a conclusion's scope.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #32 (q01590)

**Q:** A bag has 3 red, 4 blue, and 5 green marbles. What is the probability of drawing a green marble first, followed by a red marble, without replacement?

**Key:** 5/44 · **Reviewer:** 5/44 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Straightforward two-step dependent probability; closer to medium/easy than medium-high for SAT Data Analysis.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #33 (q01591)

**Q:** A fair six-sided die is rolled 3 times. What is the probability of rolling a 6 all three times?

- A) 1/18
- B) 1/36
- C) 1/216
- D) 3/6

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward application of independent probability ((1/6)^3); it is an easy-tier item rather than medium-high difficulty.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #35 (q01593)

**Q:** A survey samples 50 out of 2,000 employees and finds that 60% support a new policy. Based on this sample, about how many of the 2,000 employees would be expected to support the policy?

**Key:** 1200 · **Reviewer:** 1200 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The item only requires computing 60% of 2,000, a single-step percentage calculation typical of an easy question; it does not reflect the stated medium-high difficulty (no margin of error, sampling reasoning, or multi-step inference).

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #36 (q01594)

**Q:** Events A and B are independent, with P(A)=0.4 and P(B)=0.5. What is P(A and B)?

- A) 0.1
- B) 0.2
- C) 0.45
- D) 0.9

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: A single multiplication of independent probabilities (0.4 × 0.5) is an easy item, not medium-high difficulty; distractors (0.1, 0.45, 0.9) are not plausible traps for this computation.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #37 (q01595)

**Q:** Events A and B are mutually exclusive, with P(A)=0.3 and P(B)=0.4. What is P(A or B)?

- A) 0.12
- B) 0.3
- C) 0.4
- D) 0.7

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a one-step application of the addition rule for mutually exclusive events (0.3 + 0.4). It is an easy item, not medium-high. The distractor 0.12 (product) is the only mild trap.
- other: The phrasing 'mutually exclusive' and formal probability notation P(A or B) are outside typical Digital SAT style, which usually frames probability in context (tables, counts, proportions).

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #38 (q01596)

**Q:** Using the same mutually exclusive events (P(A)=0.3, P(B)=0.4), what is the probability that neither A nor B occurs?

**Key:** 0.3 · **Reviewer:** 0.3 (high)
- formatting: The stem begins 'Using the same mutually exclusive events,' referring to a prior question that is not included. Although the probabilities are restated in parentheses, the item is not self-contained as SAT items must be.
- difficulty_mismatch: Computing 1 - (0.3 + 0.4) for mutually exclusive events is a single-step, easy-tier task, not medium-high.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #39 (q01597)

**Q:** A class of 30 students has an average test score of 80, and another class of 20 students has an average of 90. What is the combined average score for all 50 students?

- A) 83
- B) 84
- C) 85
- D) 86

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward weighted-average computation ((30·80+20·90)/50 = 84), typically an easy-level SAT item rather than medium-high. The distractors (85 is the unweighted mean) are standard but the task requires only one step.

### Math · Data Analysis · medium-high · #40 (q01598)

**Q:** A recipe for 6 servings uses 2.5 cups of rice. How many cups of rice are needed for 15 servings, maintaining the same ratio?

- A) 5
- B) 5.5
- C) 6
- D) 6.25

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step proportion (2.5/6 = x/15), typical of an easy-tier item rather than medium-high difficulty.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #1 (q01599)

> Table: Policy Support by Age Group
> |              | Support | Oppose | Total |
> |--------------|---------|--------|-------|
> | Under 30     | 45      | 30     | 75    |
> | 30 and older | 75      | 50     | 125   |
> | Total        | 120     | 80     | 200   |

**Q:** The table below shows survey results on a policy by age group. What percent of people aged 30 and older support the policy?

- A) 50%
- B) 55%
- C) 60%
- D) 65%

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step conditional-percentage computation (75/125 = 60%), typical of an easy-tier two-way table item, not a 'hard' question. A hard version would require comparing conditional proportions or working backward from a percentage.
- formatting: The stem says 'The table below' but the table is presented above the question prompt.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #3 (q01601)

**Q:** A jar contains 6 red and 4 blue marbles. If three marbles are drawn at random without replacement, what is the probability that all three are red?

- A) 1/12
- B) 1/8
- C) 1/6
- D) 1/4

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a routine sequential-probability computation (6/10 × 5/9 × 4/8 = 1/6); it is closer to medium than hard for the SAT.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #4 (q01602)

**Q:** In a class, 50% of students are male. Among male students, 30% are on the honor roll. Among female students, 40% are on the honor roll. What percent of all students are on the honor roll?

- A) 30%
- B) 33%
- C) 35%
- D) 40%

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward weighted-average computation (0.5·30% + 0.5·40% = 35%) with equal, convenient weights; it is an easy/medium item rather than a hard one.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #6 (q01604)

**Q:** A survey of 250 people found that 40% prefer Brand A. Among those who prefer Brand A, 3/5 are under age 40. How many people surveyed are under 40 AND prefer Brand A?

- A) 50
- B) 55
- C) 60
- D) 65

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a two-step percent/fraction computation (250 × 0.40 = 100; 100 × 3/5 = 60) with small, friendly numbers — typical of an easy-tier item rather than a hard one.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #10 (q01608)

**Q:** A data set has an interquartile range (IQR) of 20 and Q1=70. What is Q3?

**Key:** 90 · **Reviewer:** 90 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a one-step definition recall (Q3 = Q1 + IQR), far easier than a 'hard' SAT Data Analysis item.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #11 (q01609)

**Q:** A committee of 3 people is randomly selected from a group of 5 men and 4 women (9 people total). What is the probability that all 3 selected are women?

- A) 1/84
- B) 1/21
- C) 1/9
- D) 4/9

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Straightforward combinations probability; closer to medium than hard.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #13 (q01611)

**Q:** How many total ways can a 3-person committee be selected from a group of 9 people?

**Key:** 84 · **Reviewer:** 84 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step application of the combination formula C(9,3)=84, which would be an easy item, not 'hard.' Moreover, counting/combinatorics is not part of the SAT's Problem-Solving and Data Analysis domain, so the item is off-specification for the stated skill.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #14 (q01612)

**Q:** A class of 40 students has an average test score of 78. Some students averaged 75, and the rest averaged 85. How many students averaged 85?

- A) 10
- B) 12
- C) 15
- D) 18

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a routine weighted-average/mixture setup solvable in one equation; it is closer to medium than hard for SAT Data Analysis.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #16 (q01614)

**Q:** A line of best fit for a scatterplot is given by y = 3x + 5. For an actual data point (4, 20), what is the residual (actual y minus predicted y)?

- A) -3
- B) 0
- C) 3
- D) 17

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step computation (predicted value 17, residual 20-17=3) typical of an easy item, not a 'hard' tier question.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #17 (q01615)

**Q:** Which statement about correlation and causation is most accurate?

- A) A strong correlation always implies causation
- B) Correlation can suggest a relationship but does not by itself establish causation
- C) Causation always produces a correlation coefficient of exactly 1
- D) If two variables are not correlated, one cannot possibly cause the other

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a trivial definitional recall item; the correct choice is obvious and the distractors are absolute statements ('always', 'cannot possibly') that are easily eliminated. It does not match a 'hard' tier, and the SAT typically assesses correlation/causation in the context of a described study or data set rather than as an abstract definition.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #18 (q01616)

**Q:** A poll estimates support for a measure at 48%, with a margin of error of 4 percentage points. What is the lowest plausible value, in percent, for the true level of support?

**Key:** 44 · **Reviewer:** 44 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single subtraction (48 − 4); it is an easy item, not a 'hard' one.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #20 (q01618)

**Q:** A survey of 500 adults estimates that 55% support a policy, with a margin of error of ±3 percentage points. A second, separate survey of 500 different adults using the same methodology estimates 51% support. Are these results necessarily contradictory?

- A) Yes, because the point estimates differ
- B) No, because their margins of error overlap (52%-58% and 48%-54%), so both could reflect a similar true value
- C) Yes, because a sample size of 500 is too small to trust either result
- D) No, because any two percentages under 60% are always considered statistically equal

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Labeled 'hard,' but the item is conceptually straightforward: the three distractors are transparently false (A ignores sampling error, C is an unsupported blanket claim, D is an absurd overgeneralization), and the correct choice even supplies the computed intervals. This plays as an easy/medium question.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #21 (q01619)

**Q:** A drink mix is 25% concentrate and 75% water by volume. How many liters of concentrate are needed to make 20 liters of the drink using this ratio?

**Key:** 5 · **Reviewer:** 5 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step percent-of-a-total computation (0.25 × 20 = 5), typical of an easy-tier item; it lacks the multi-step reasoning expected of a 'hard' Data Analysis question.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #23 (q01621)

**Q:** A company tracks defect rates across three factories: Factory A produces 1,000 units with a 2% defect rate, Factory B produces 1,500 units with a 3% defect rate, and Factory C produces 500 units with a 1% defect rate. What is the overall defect rate across all three factories, rounded to the nearest tenth of a percent?

- A) 2.0%
- B) 2.2%
- C) 2.3%
- D) 2.5%

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Labeled 'hard,' but the item only requires computing 20+45+5=70 defects out of 3,000 units (2.33% → 2.3%), a routine weighted-average calculation typical of easy/medium SAT data-analysis items. The distractor 2.0% (unweighted mean) is the only real trap.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #25 (q01623)

**Q:** A data set has a mean of 40 and a standard deviation of 5. If every value is decreased by 10 and then multiplied by 3, what is the new standard deviation?

- A) 5
- B) 15
- C) 25
- D) 45

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The item is a single-step application of the rule that adding/subtracting a constant leaves standard deviation unchanged while multiplying scales it; this is typically easy-to-medium, not hard.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #28 (q01626)

**Q:** A survey of 300 people found that the ratio of "agree" to "disagree" to "neutral" responses is 5:3:2. How many people responded "neutral"?

- A) 30
- B) 60
- C) 90
- D) 150

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward one-step ratio partition (300 ÷ 10 × 2); it is an easy-tier item, not hard.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #29 (q01627)

**Q:** Using the same survey (300 people, ratio 5:3:2 for agree:disagree:neutral), what percent of respondents disagree?

- A) 20%
- B) 25%
- C) 30%
- D) 35%

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a one-step ratio-to-percent conversion (3/10 = 30%); it is an easy-level item, not 'hard.' The 300-person figure is extraneous.
- formatting: The stem begins 'Using the same survey,' referring to a previous question that is not provided. Although the needed data is restated parenthetically, the dangling reference is not SAT-standard; items should be self-contained.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #30 (q01628)

**Q:** A student needs an overall average of at least 90 across 4 equally weighted tests. If the first three test scores are 85, 92, and 88, what is the minimum score needed on the 4th test?

**Key:** 95 · **Reviewer:** 95 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a routine one-step average computation (360 - 265 = 95), typical of an easy-tier item, not a 'hard' Data Analysis question.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #31 (q01629)

**Q:** A data set of 8 numbers has a mean of 15. Two additional numbers, 20 and 25, are added to the set. What is the new mean of all 10 numbers?

- A) 15.5
- B) 16
- C) 16.5
- D) 17

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a routine one-step mean computation (sum 8×15=120, add 45, divide by 10 = 16.5). It would be an easy-tier item, not hard.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #35 (q01633)

**Q:** Holding all else constant, the margin of error of a survey estimate is most closely related to which of the following?

- A) The sample size
- B) The size of the total population being studied
- C) The specific topic of the survey
- D) The day of the week the survey was conducted

**Key:** A · **Reviewer:** A (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The item is labeled 'hard' but is a straightforward recall question with three obviously irrelevant distractors (survey topic, day of week, population size); a typical hard Data Analysis item would require comparing two studies' sample sizes/variability or reasoning about how margin of error changes quantitatively.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #36 (q01634)

**Q:** Store A sold 100 items in Q1 and 120 items in Q2. Store B sold 150 items in Q1 and 180 items in Q2. What is the percent increase in total items sold (both stores combined) from Q1 to Q2?

**Key:** 20 · **Reviewer:** 20 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The computation (250 → 300, a 20% increase) is a single straightforward percent-change calculation, far easier than a 'hard' tier item; both stores happen to grow by the same 20%, removing any complexity.

### Math · Data Analysis · hard · #40 (q01638)

**Q:** A data set has a mean of 10 and a standard deviation of 4. Every value in the data set is transformed using the rule y = 2x - 3. What are the new mean and standard deviation?

- A) mean=17, SD=5
- B) mean=17, SD=8
- C) mean=20, SD=8
- D) mean=20, SD=5

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a straightforward application of linear transformation rules on mean and standard deviation; it is closer to medium than hard.

### Math · Geometry · easy · #5 (q01643)

**Q:** What is the sum of the interior angles of a triangle?

- A) 90 degrees
- B) 180 degrees
- C) 270 degrees
- D) 360 degrees

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a pure recall fact well below even the easiest SAT geometry item; the SAT does not ask bare definitional questions like this without any application or figure.

### Math · Geometry · easy · #17 (q01655)

**Q:** Which type of triangle has all three sides equal in length?

- A) Scalene
- B) Isosceles
- C) Equilateral
- D) Right

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a pure definition-recall item with no computation or reasoning; even 'easy' Digital SAT geometry questions require applying a property (e.g., finding a side length or angle measure). The item is well below the lowest SAT difficulty tier and is not SAT-style.

### Math · Geometry · easy · #31 (q01669)

**Q:** What type of angle measures exactly 90 degrees?

- A) Acute
- B) Right
- C) Obtuse
- D) Straight

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a pure vocabulary recall item far below even the easiest SAT geometry question; the SAT does not test definitions of angle types in isolation and requires some computation or reasoning.

### Math · Geometry · easy · #35 (q01673)

**Q:** How many degrees are in a straight angle?

- A) 90 degrees
- B) 180 degrees
- C) 270 degrees
- D) 360 degrees

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Even at the 'easy' tier, the SAT does not test bare definitional recall like this; authentic easy geometry items require at least one computational or reasoning step (e.g., finding an unknown angle on a line). This item is well below the test's floor.

### Math · Geometry · medium-low · #4 (q01682)

**Q:** Two parallel lines are cut by a transversal. If one angle measures 65 degrees, what is the measure of its corresponding angle?

- A) 25 degrees
- B) 65 degrees
- C) 115 degrees
- D) 180 degrees

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct recall of the corresponding-angles postulate with no computation; it is easier than typical SAT 'medium-low' geometry items, which usually require at least one algebraic or multi-step inference.

### Math · Geometry · medium-low · #6 (q01684)

**Q:** A circle has a radius of 8. What is the length of an arc that subtends a central angle of 90 degrees? (Express your answer in terms of π.)

**Key:** 4π · **Reviewer:** 4π (high)
- formatting: Student-produced response (grid-in) answers on the Digital SAT cannot contain symbols such as π; the answer 4π is unenterable. The item should either be multiple choice or ask for the coefficient of π (or a decimal approximation).

### Math · Geometry · medium-low · #22 (q01700)

**Q:** In a coordinate plane, a line passes through (2,3) and (6,11). What is the slope of the line?

- A) 1
- B) 2
- C) 3
- D) 4

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- other: Minor mislabeling: slope of a line through two points is typically classified under Algebra (linear equations/functions) rather than Geometry on the Digital SAT.

### Math · Geometry · medium-low · #26 (q01704)

**Q:** Using the same square (diagonal of 10) inscribed in the circle, what is the area of the square?

- A) 25
- B) 50
- C) 75
- D) 100

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The stem references "the same square ... inscribed in the circle," implying a prior question/figure that is not provided. The parenthetical (diagonal of 10) does supply the needed data, so the item is still solvable, but the dangling reference is sloppy and non-standard for SAT items, which must be self-contained.

### Math · Geometry · medium-low · #30 (q01708)

**Q:** Using the same triangle with vertices (0,0), (4,0), and (0,3), what is the length of the side connecting (4,0) and (0,3)?

**Key:** 5 · **Reviewer:** 5 (high)
- other: The phrase "Using the same triangle" references a prior item that is not present; SAT items are standalone. The vertices are restated, so the question is still solvable, but the lead-in should be removed.

### Math · Geometry · medium-low · #32 (q01710)

**Q:** Which statement is true about vertical angles?

- A) They are always supplementary
- B) They are always complementary
- C) They are always congruent
- D) They are always right angles

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a pure definition-recall item ('vertical angles are congruent') with no computation or application. It is easier than a 'medium-low' SAT geometry item and does not match the SAT's style, which embeds such facts in a diagram- or equation-based problem.

### Math · Geometry · medium-low · #33 (q01711)

**Q:** A circle's sector has a central angle of 45 degrees and a radius of 8. What is the area of the sector? (Express your answer in terms of π.)

**Key:** 8π · **Reviewer:** 8π (high)
- formatting: Student-produced response fields on the Digital SAT accept only numerals, a decimal point, and a slash — π cannot be entered. The question should either be multiple choice or ask for the coefficient of π (or the area to a numeric value).

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #1 (q01719)

**Q:** In a circle, a central angle measures 80 degrees. What is the measure of the inscribed angle that subtends the same arc?

- A) 20 degrees
- B) 40 degrees
- C) 80 degrees
- D) 160 degrees

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step application of the inscribed angle theorem (half the central angle) with no additional reasoning, computation, or figure interpretation. It is an easy-tier item rather than medium-high.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #2 (q01720)

**Q:** A right triangle has an angle of 30 degrees, and the side opposite that angle has length 5. What is the length of the hypotenuse?

- A) 5
- B) 5√3
- C) 10
- D) 10√3

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct application of the 30-60-90 ratio (hypotenuse = 2 × side opposite 30°), which is an easy-tier item rather than medium-high.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #4 (q01722)

**Q:** A tangent line to a circle touches the circle at point P. If a radius is drawn to point P, what is the measure of the angle between the tangent line and the radius?

- A) 0 degrees
- B) 45 degrees
- C) 90 degrees
- D) 180 degrees

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct recall of a basic theorem (tangent ⟂ radius) with no computation or reasoning steps; it is far easier than a 'medium-high' difficulty SAT geometry item, and the distractors (0°, 180°) are implausible.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #7 (q01725)

**Q:** A circle has the equation (x-3)^2 + (y+2)^2 = 16. What is the radius of the circle?

- A) 3
- B) 4
- C) 8
- D) 16

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct recall application of the standard circle equation (r = √16 = 4), typical of an easy-tier item, not medium-high difficulty. No multi-step reasoning, completing the square, or interpretation is required.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #9 (q01727)

**Q:** A cylinder has a lateral surface area of 120π and a height of 10. What is its radius?

**Key:** 6 · **Reviewer:** 6 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step application of the lateral surface area formula; it is easier than a typical medium-high item.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #10 (q01728)

**Q:** In a right triangle, angle A = 45 degrees, and the hypotenuse has length 8√2. What is the length of each leg?

- A) 4
- B) 4√2
- C) 8
- D) 8√2

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct application of the 45-45-90 special right triangle ratio with friendly numbers (8√2 ÷ √2 = 8); it is an easy-level item rather than medium-high.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #13 (q01731)

**Q:** A regular polygon has an interior angle of 144 degrees. How many sides does it have?

- A) 8
- B) 9
- C) 10
- D) 12

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step application of the exterior-angle relationship (180 − 144 = 36; 360/36 = 10). It is an easy-to-medium item, not medium-high.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #14 (q01732)

**Q:** A right triangle has legs in the ratio 3:4, and its hypotenuse is 20. What is the length of the longer leg?

- A) 12

- D) 18

- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct recognition of the 3-4-5 triple scaled by 4; it is an easy-tier item, not medium-high.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #15 (q01733)

**Q:** Using the same right triangle (legs in ratio 3:4, hypotenuse 20), what is the length of the shorter leg?

**Key:** 12 · **Reviewer:** 12 (high)
- formatting: The stem opens with "Using the same right triangle...", referencing a prior question that is not provided. Although the needed information (legs in ratio 3:4, hypotenuse 20) is restated, the dangling reference is a standalone-item defect; SAT items must be self-contained.
- difficulty_mismatch: Recognizing a 3-4-5 triangle scaled by 4 is a routine, low-difficulty task, not medium-high.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #16 (q01734)

- difficulty_mismatch: Direct one-step application of V = (4/3)\u03c0r^3 with a convenient radius; this is an easy item, not medium-high.

- difficulty_mismatch: Finding the edge (cube root of 216 = 6) and surface area (6 × 36 = 216) is a routine one-step-plus computation, typical of an easy item rather than medium-high difficulty.

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #32 (q01750)

**Q:** A circle has center (2, -3) and passes through the point (2, 5). What is the radius of the circle?

### Math · Geometry · medium-high · #33 (q01751)
