- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step application of the similar-figures area ratio rule with no context or computation; it is an easy item, not medium-high.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #1 (q01759)

**Q:** A sphere is inscribed in a cube with side length 8 (the sphere touches the center of each face). What is the volume of the sphere, in terms of π?

- A) 64π/3
- B) 128π/3
- C) 256π/3
- D) 512π/3

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The item is a one-step application of the sphere volume formula (r = 4, V = 256π/3), which is easy-to-medium rather than hard.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #2 (q01760)

**Q:** Using the same sphere inscribed in a cube of side length 8, what is the ratio of the sphere's volume to the cube's volume?

- A) π/8
- B) π/6
- C) π/4
- D) π/3

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- formatting: The stem opens with "Using the same sphere inscribed in a cube of side length 8", referencing a previous item that is not shown. The needed information is restated, so the item is still solvable, but the dangling reference is sloppy for a standalone question.
- difficulty_mismatch: Computing (4/3)\u03c0(4)^3 / 8^3 = \u03c0/6 is a routine one-step volume-ratio calculation, well below the 'hard' tier; the ratio is also the standard memorizable result \u03c0/6 for any inscribed sphere.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #4 (q01762)

**Q:** A triangle has sides a=7 and b=8, with an included angle C=60°. Using the Law of Cosines, what is the length of side c?

- A) √41
- B) √57
- C) √73
- D) √113

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- other: The Law of Cosines is not part of the Digital SAT Math content domain (SAT trigonometry is limited to right-triangle ratios, radians, and the unit-circle basics). The item is off-syllabus even though it is mathematically sound.
- difficulty_mismatch: Labeled 'hard,' but the task is a single direct substitution into a formula given in the prompt; among students who know the formula it is routine.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #5 (q01763)

**Q:** A regular hexagon has a side length of 6. What is its area?

- A) 36√3
- B) 54√3
- C) 72√3
- D) 108√3

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a direct application of the standard regular-hexagon area formula (3√3/2)s² with no additional reasoning steps; it is an easy/medium item rather than a hard one.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #7 (q01765)

**Q:** From a point outside a circle, a tangent segment has length 12, and a secant from the same point has an external segment of length 8 and a total length of x. Using the relationship tangent^2 = (external segment)(whole secant), what is x?

- A) 14
- B) 16
- C) 18
- D) 20

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The question states the needed formula explicitly, reducing it to solving 144 = 8x; this is easier than a 'hard' SAT geometry item.
- other: Geometrically inconsistent: with tangent 12 and external segment 8, the whole secant must be 18, so the chord portion is 10 — this is fine, but the given external segment 8 < tangent 12 is required and holds, so no real error. (Minor: no diagram, but values are consistent.)

### Math · Geometry · hard · #8 (q01766)

**Q:** A right circular cone has a height of 12 and a base radius of 5. What is the slant height of the cone?

- A) 10
- B) 11
- C) 12
- D) 13

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step Pythagorean theorem application with a familiar 5-12-13 triple; it is an easy-tier item, not hard.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #10 (q01768)

**Q:** A triangle has vertices A(0,0), B(6,0), and C(3,4). Based on its side lengths, what type of triangle is it?

- A) Equilateral
- B) Isosceles
- C) Scalene
- D) Right

**Key:** B · **Reviewer:** B (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: Computing AB=6, AC=5, BC=5 and identifying the triangle as isosceles is a very routine distance-formula application, far easier than a 'hard' SAT geometry item.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #11 (q01769)

**Q:** Two chords of a circle intersect at point P inside the circle. One chord is divided into segments of 6 and x. The other chord is divided into segments of 4 and 9. What is the value of x?

- A) 4
- B) 5
- C) 6
- D) 8

**Key:** C · **Reviewer:** C (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a single-step application of the intersecting chords theorem (6x = 4·9, x = 6) with no additional reasoning, figure interpretation, or algebraic complexity. It is an easy-level item, not 'hard.'

### Math · Geometry · hard · #12 (q01770)

**Q:** A right triangle has legs 6 and 8 (and hypotenuse 10) and an inradius of 2. Using the formula Area = r x s, where s is the semi-perimeter, what is the area of the triangle?

**Key:** 24 · **Reviewer:** 24 (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: The question hands the student the formula, the inradius, and all three side lengths, so it reduces to computing s = 12 and multiplying 2 x 12. This is a one-step arithmetic task, far easier than a 'hard' SAT geometry item.
- other: The item is self-verifying/trivially redundant: the area of a 6-8-10 right triangle (24) can be found directly as (1/2)(6)(8) without using the given formula, so it tests little.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #14 (q01772)

### Math · Geometry · hard · #15 (q01773)

### Math · Geometry · hard · #16 (q01774)

- D) 91

- difficulty_mismatch: Straightforward direct application of a named formula; closer to easy/medium than hard.
- other: Heron's formula is not part of the SAT's tested content domain, making this somewhat off-spec for the Digital SAT.

### Math · Geometry · hard · #36 (q01794)

**Key:** 8 · **Reviewer:** 8 (high)

### Math · Geometry · hard · #40 (q01798)

**Q:** A regular polygon has an interior angle to exterior angle ratio of 5:1. How many sides does the polygon have?

- A) 9
- B) 10
- C) 11
- D) 12

**Key:** D · **Reviewer:** D (high)
- difficulty_mismatch: This is a routine two-step computation (interior + exterior = 180° → exterior = 30° → n = 360/30 = 12), typically an easy/medium SAT geometry item rather than a 'hard' one.
